tool: Write {"content": "# Brief: config-file-sync\n\n## Problem\nDevelopers edit `.troupe/` files directly, in their editor, while troupe is running. Today those edits never\nreach the history: step 1 records an import once and then never reconciles (\"importing is not reconciling\").\nSo `history` silently misses every hand edit, and nothing notices edits as they happen.\n\n## Current State\n- config-foundation: `open_theatre` loads the files (`load_config` → `ConfigDocument`, whose `files` lists the\n  root, YAML includes and text includes), opens `HistoryStore` (`<theatre>/.troupe/history.sqlite`), and imports\n  once (`ensure_imported`). The `ConfigHistory` aggregate holds no derived leaf state; there is no projection of\n  \"the configuration as recorded\". `flatten_leaves` gives leaves in document order; `scalar_equal` compares typed\n  values.\n- config-set-writeback: `set_value` diffs before/after documents with `diff_leaves` (sets in after-order, then\n  removes in before-order), writes files atomically (`write_atomically`: hidden `.<name>.troupe-tmp-<hex>` temp\n  file next to the target, then `os.replace`), then records events via `HistoryStore.record_changes`.\n- CLI: subcommands registered through `SUBCOMMAND_REGISTRARS` in `cli/main.py`; handlers write into a buffer\n  that is flushed only on success. This does not fit a long-running foreground command.\n- No locking between processes. No file-watching dependency (runtime deps: eventsourcing, ruamel.yaml).\n- Gap: no reconcile, no `watch`, nothing that stops a `set`'s write-back being seen as a new change.\n\n## Desired Outcome\nThe step 2 contract section \"Sync with files\" holds:\n- `troupe config watch --theatre <dir>` runs in the foreground until SIGINT or SIGTERM, then exits 0. Once it can\n  observe changes, it prints exactly `watching <dir>\\n` to stdout and flushes. Any change made after that\n  line, to the config file or to an included file, is reconciled within 2 seconds.\n- **Reconcile** compares the...[truncated 8224 chars]

Dependencies: config-foundation\\n\"\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old, old+\"- [ ] config-file-sync -- (step 2) reconcile files against the recorded configuration (set/remove per differing leaf), reconcile-first for every command except `history`, `troupe config watch` (polling, readiness line, include following, SIGINT/SIGTERM → 0), inter-process lock so a `set`'s write-back is never re-recorded. Dependencies: config-foundation, config-set-writeback\\n\")\ns+='''\n## Step 2 — Sync with files (2026-10-02)\n\n### Overview\nPeople edit `.troupe/` files in their editor while troupe runs. Step 2 adds the contract section \"Sync with\nfiles\": `troupe config watch --theatre <dir>` reconciles hand edits within 2 s, every command except `history`\nreconciles first, and troupe's own write-back is never recorded a second time. This supersedes step 1's\n\"importing is not reconciling\" for every command except `history`. Decision A (files are the truth; show/set\nread the files) still holds.\n\n### Approach Decision\n- **Chosen**: reconcile = diff the loaded files' leaves against a projection of the *recorded* configuration\n  (fold of import/set/remove), recorded as `set`/`remove` events in one transaction. `watch` polls the watched\n  files' contents with the stdlib (watch set = `ConfigDocument.files` + config file candidates, recomputed\n  after each load). An exclusive inter-process lock (no leftover file, e.g. `flock` on `.troupe/`) serialises\n  each reconcile and each `set` (reconcile + write + record).\n- **Why**: diffing against what was recorded is idempotent. Edits made while nothing ran are found, and a\n  `set`'s write-back finds nothing new, with no self-write bookkeeping. The lock closes the gap ...[truncated 1764 chars]

That reverses step 1's 'importing is not reconciling', so the\nroadmap records the new spec, the approach (diff against the recorded configuration,\nstdlib polling, an inter-process lock), and the existing-spec amendments.\" && git log --oneline -1 && git status --short"}
assistant: I've finished discovery and committed it on `main` (`8f58c05`). This covers planning only: no code for `watch` or reconcile has been written yet. If the step is tested as delivered behaviour, those tests will fail until the spec is implemented.\n\n**Path: mixed.** Step 2 needs one new spec, `config-file-sync`. It also reverses a step-1 rule in both existing specs: edits made by hand used to add no history events ("importing is not reconciling"), and now every command except `history` must record them. The rule that `show` and `set` read the files on disk stays as it is.\n\n**Chosen approach** (recorded in `spec/spec/config-file-sync/brief.md`):\n- **Reconcile** compares the leaves in the files with the configuration as recorded, which is worked out by replaying the history. It records a `set` or `remove` event for each leaf that differs, all in one transaction. Edits that only change formatting or comments produce no events.\n- **A `set`'s own write-back is never recorded twice.** The `set` has already recorded those values, so the next reconcile finds nothing to add.\n- **`set` and `watch` take turns through a lock** held on `.troupe/`, so no extra file is left behind. Without it, `watch` could reconcile between a `set` writing the file and recording its events, which would duplicate them.\n- **`watch` checks file contents every few hundred milliseconds**, using only the standard library. It watches the config file and every included file, and re-reads the list after each load so a newly added include is followed. It takes its first snapshot before printing `watching <dir>`, so the line is true when it appears.\n- **I didn't use a file-watching library** (watchfiles or watchdog). It would add a dependency and make the "only print once watching" promise harder to keep.\n\n**Open choices**, listed in the brief for the requirements step:\n- If the files can't be loaded during `watch` (for example, a half-saved file), it records nothing and keeps watching. It never treats that as every leaf being removed.\n- `<dir>` in the `watching` line is printed exactly as it was given.\n- `watch` on a theatre that has never been imported imports it first.\n- `watch` prints only the `watching` line to stdout; everything else goes to stderr.\n\n**Roadmap** (`spec/steering/roadmap.md`): I kept all the step-1 content and added a step-2 section. It lists `config-file-sync` as a new spec, plus requirement changes to `config-foundation` (6.9 and 7.3) and `config-set-writeback` (the 15.1 example). The step-1 tests that assert hand edits add no events will be updated within the new spec's tasks, in the same change as the new behaviour.\n\n**Next steps:**\n- `/method-command config-file-sync`, or `/method-command config-file-sync` to go straight through to tasks.\n- `/method-command config-foundation` and `/method-command config-set-writeback` to update the step-1 requirements this reverses.

Once it\n  is watching, it prints one line `watching <dir>` to stdout and flushes. The line is a promise: it is printed\n  only after `watch` is able to observe changes. Within 2 seconds of any change to the config file or an\n  included file made after that line appears, it reconciles.\n- **Reconcile:** compare the files with the configuration and emit a `set`/`remove` event for each leaf that\n  differs. A formatting-only or comment-only edit emits nothing.\n- Every other command except `history` reconciles first, so edits made while nothing was running are picked\n  up. `history` never reconciles: it reports what has been recorded.\n- A `set` while `watch` runs emits exactly the `set`'s own events; its write-back produces no further events.\n- An include added by a hand edit is followed; its leaves appear as `set` events.'''\ndesc=\"\\n\\n\".join([\"### Problem\\n\"+sec(\"Problem\"),\"### Current State\\n\"+sec(\"Current State\"),\"### Desired Outcome\\n\"+sec(\"Desired Outcome\"),contract,\"See `brief.md` for the chosen approach, scope, boundaries and open choices.\"])\nt=open('../../../spec/settings/templates/spec/requirements-init.md').read().replace('{{PROJECT_DESCRIPTION}}',desc)\nopen('requirements.md','w').write(t)\nj=open('../../../spec/settings/templates/spec/init.json').read().replace('{{FEATURE_NAME}}','config-file-sync').replace('{{TIMESTAMP}}','2026-10-02T22:13:35Z')\nopen('spec.json','w').write(j)\nEOF\npython3 -m json.tool spec.json >/dev/null && head -12 requirements.md && cd /work/repo && git add spec && git commit -q -m \"spec(config-file-sync): initialise the step 2 sync...[truncated 216 chars]

assistant: ## Generated Requirements Summary\nI wrote seven requirements:\n- **1. Reconcile first:** `show` and `set` reconcile before doing their own work, and keep the events even if they then exit 3 or 5. `history` never reconciles, though it still imports a theatre that has never been imported. A usage error or load failure records nothing. A hand edit made before a `set` is recorded ahead of the `set`'s own event.\n- **2. Reconcile events:** a changed leaf gets `set` with the recorded value as `old`. A new leaf gets `set` with `old` null, and a leaf no longer in the files gets `remove`. Edits to formatting, comments or quoting record nothing, but a change of type does. All events from one reconcile are saved together or not at all, with consecutive `seq`: `set` events first, then `remove` events.\n- **3. Includes added or removed by hand:** their leaves are recorded, and `watch` starts or stops following those files.\n- **4–5. `watch`:** it uses the same default theatre as the other commands. It prints `watching <dir>` only once a change is certain to be seen, and reconciles within 2 seconds of a change. It also notices files saved by rename and `troupe.yml`↔`troupe.yaml` renames. SIGINT or SIGTERM exits 0, and a startup failure exits 4 under the step 1 rules.\n- **6–7. Write-back and clean-up:** a `set` running alongside `watch` records exactly its own events. Each hand edit is recorded once, and events from two commands never interleave. Nothing new is left behind, and the history store is closed when a command ends.\n\nWhere the contract is silent, I made these choices; each is listed in the requirements and still needs recording in `research.md`:\n- If the files can't be loaded while `watch` runs, it records nothing, reports the problem and keeps running. It never treats that as every leaf being removed.\n- If the history store fails while `watch` runs, it reports the failure and retries later.\n- Edits made in quick succession may be recorded as their net difference.\n- `<dir>` is printed as it was given.\n\nThe Boundary Context section lists the step 1 criteria this replaces: config-foundation 6.9 and 7.3. It also notes the changed example in config-set-writeback 15.1.\n\n## Document Status\n- `spec/spec/config-file-sync/requirements.md` is written.\n- `spec/spec/config-file-sync/spec.json` is at phase `requirements-generated`, with requirements marked generated but not approved.\n- Both are committed on `main` (`28e738a`).\n\n## Review Gate\nThe draft passed after one repair pass. Every heading has a numeric ID and every criterion has the EARS form. There are no technology terms in the requirements. The repair rewrote 5.5 and 6.1 into EARS form and gave a precise ordering rule for `remove` events (2.6).\n\n## Next Steps\nReview the requirements, especially the silent-contract choices. Then optionally run a gap analysis before moving to design:\n```\n/method-command config-file-sync\n/method-command config-file-sync\n```

Reconcile in core would import from `writeback`, or the function moves to a shared module. |\n| Leaf diff | `writeback/leaf_diff.diff_leaves(before: ConfigDocument, after: ConfigDocument)` gives `SetChange`s in after-order, then `RemoveChange`s in before-order. | The algorithm shape fits reconcile. | It takes two `ConfigDocument`s. Reconcile's \"before\" is the recorded configuration (a path to value ...[truncated 15495 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; git add spec && git commit -q -m \"research(config-file-sync): analyse the gap between sync requirements and the code\n\nRecords what reconcile and watch can reuse (open_theatre, document.files, scalar_equal,\nrecord_changes), and what is missing. That includes a verified failure: two processes\nappending to the history collide with an IntegrityError, so a lock is required.\" && git log --oneline -1"}
assistant: ## Analysis Summary\nThe requirements are generated but not approved yet; I ran the analysis anyway because it can still shape them.\n\n- **Reconcile is small.** Most of it already exists: `open_theatre` is the one place every command passes through, `document.files` lists every included file, `flatten_leaves` and `scalar_equal` give the leaves and the typed comparison, and `record_changes` already saves a batch in one transaction. What's missing is a way to compute the configuration as recorded from the history. I checked that values like `inf`, `nan`, `1.0` and `true` read back from the history with the right types, so the comparison will work.\n- **A lock is required.** I tested two processes adding to the history at once, and the second one fails with `IntegrityError` (it would exit 4). Nothing gets corrupted, but a `set` would fail spuriously while `watch` runs, and exactly-once recording breaks. A `set` also needs the lock across its file write and its event record, or `watch` could record its write-back as a hand edit.\n- **`watch` is the biggest piece.** The CLI currently holds all output until a command finishes, which can't work for the `watching` line. SIGTERM currently kills the process and SIGINT prints a traceback. There is no file watching and no test helper for a background process.\n- **Six existing tests assert the old "hand edits add no events" behaviour.** They're listed in `research.md` and must change together with the code.\n- **Recommended approach (option C):** new modules for the recorded configuration, reconcile, the lock and the watch loop, plus a `watch` subcommand. `open_theatre` would take the lock and reconcile by default, and only `history` would opt out, so a future command can't silently skip reconciling. Effort is medium (3–7 days) and risk is medium (concurrency, signals and timing-based tests).\n- **Questions carried to design:** which lock, how `watch` notices changes, and how signals are handled. Also: what to watch while the files can't be loaded, how to report errors without repeating them, printing `<dir>` exactly as given (`--theatre` is parsed as a `Path`, which rewrites `./t/` as `t`), the order of `remove` events, and how to keep timing tests reliable.\n\n## Document Status\nI wrote the analysis to `spec/spec/config-file-sync/research.md`, a new file. It covers what exists, a map from each requirement to what's missing, the three options with trade-offs, effort and risk, and the open questions for design. It's committed on `main` (`1ba03be`). No code changed.\n\n## Next Steps\nOnce you approve the requirements, run:\n```\n/method-command config-file-sync\n```\nOr `/method-command config-file-sync -y` approves the requirements and goes straight to design.

## arm-a1-005

The project rules ask for a human review at each phase, so I didn't treat running the design step as approval.\n\nBefore approving, the requirements worth checking are the choices I made where the step 2 contract says nothing:\n- **5.3:** while `watch` runs, files that can't be loaded record no events, never "every leaf removed". `watch` reports the error and keeps running.\n- **5.4:** if the history store fails during `watch`, it reports the error and retries; it doesn't exit.\n- **5.5:** edits made in quick succession may be recorded as one net difference.\n- **4.3:** `<dir>` in the `watching` line is printed exactly as given on the command line.\n- **2.6:** within one reconcile, `set` events come first in the files' order, then `remove` events in the order those leaves were created in the history.\n- **Boundary Context:** step 2 replaces config-foundation 6.9 and 7.3. A hand edit is now recorded by every command except `history`.\n\nTo approve the requirements as they stand and continue to design, run:\n```\n/method-command config-file-sync -y\n```\nTo change something first, tell me what, and I'll update `requirements.md` and re-run the review gate.

tool: Write {"content": "# Design Document: config-file-sync\n\n## Overview\n**Purpose**: This feature keeps a theatre's recorded configuration history in step with the files developers\nedit by hand. It adds a **reconcile** that compares the files with the configuration as recorded and appends one\n`set` or `remove` event per differing leaf. Every `troupe config` command except `history` reconciles first, and\nthe new `troupe config watch` reconciles continuously, within 2 seconds of each change.\n\n**Users**: Developers who edit `.troupe/` files in their editor while troupe runs, and who read\n`troupe config history` to see what changed.\n\n**Impact**: This replaces step 1's \"importing is not reconciling\" for every command except `history`\n(config-foundation 6.9 and 7.3). Product-owner decision A is unchanged: `show` and `set` still read the files as\non disk. What changes is that the history now also records hand edits. Each command now holds an exclusive\nper-theatre lock while it runs, so a `set`'s write-back and a concurrent reconcile can never record the same\nchange twice.\n\n### Goals\n- Reconcile leaf by leaf against the recorded configuration, with typed comparison, atomic recording and\n  deterministic order (2.1–2.9, 3.1–3.3).\n- Reconcile first in every command except `history`, using one code path shared with `watch` (1.1–1.7).\n- `watch`: an honest readiness line, changes recorded within 2 s, includes followed, tolerance of broken files\n  and store failures, and a clean exit 0 on SIGINT/SIGTERM (3.4–3.5, 4.1–4.9, 5.1–5.7).\n- Each change recorded exactly once across concurrent commands, and no leftover files (6.1–6.4, 7.1–7.3).\n\n### Non-Goals\n- Any change to how `set` renders and writes values, or to `show`/`history` output formats.\n- New event kinds (reconcile reuses `set`/`remove`).\n- Native file-system notifications, or any new runtime dependency.\n- Concurrent writers to the same file during a `set` (still out of contract), Windows, anchors and alia...[truncated 31170 chars]

',line)\n    if m and cur: ids.append(f\"{cur}.{m.group(1)}\")\nd=open('design.md').read()\nfound=set(re.findall(r'\\b(\\d+\\.\\d+)\\b',d))\nfor a,b in re.findall(r'\\b(\\d+\\.\\d+)[–-](\\d+\\.\\d+)\\b',d):\n    x,y=a.split('.');_,z=b.split('.')\n    for i in range(int(y),int(z)+1): found.add(f\"{x}.{i}\")\nfor a in re.findall(r'\\b(\\d+)\\.x\\b',d):\n    found|={i for i in ids if i.startswith(a+'.')}\nprint(len(ids),'missing:',[i for i in ids if i not in found])\nEOF\ngrep -n \"TBD\\|TODO\" design.md; wc -l design.md"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/spec/spec/config-file-sync; cat >> research.md <<'EOF'\n\n---\n\n## Design Discovery & Decisions (2026-10-02)\n\n### Summary\n- **Feature**: `config-file-sync`\n- **Discovery Scope**: Extension (light discovery). No new external dependency; integration-focused.\n- **Key Findings**:\n  - Concurrent appends from two processes fail with `IntegrityError`, so a theatre lock is required for 6.x\n    and for the `set` write-back window.\n  - The loader exposes every file read (`ConfigDocument.files`, display `path` + exact `text`), so the watch set\n    comes for free after each successful load.\n  - Typed values round-trip through the store, so a fold of `records()` gives a recorded configuration that\n    compares correctly with `scalar_equal`.\n- **Skills consulted**: method-command rules (principles, light discovery, synthesis, review gate). No other\n  domain skill applies (CLI and data, no UI).\n\n### Decision: Reconcile runs inside `open_theatre` (opt-out for `history`)\n- **Context**: 1.1–1.5 require every command except `history` to reconcile first; `watch` must reconcile the same\n  way.\n- **Alternatives**: (1) each handler calls reconcile explicitly; (2) a separate opener per mode;\n  (3) a default-on flag in `open_theatre`.\n- **Selected**: (3). `open_theatre(directory, *, reconcile=True)`; only `handle_history` passes `False`.\n  `watch` syncs are `with open_theatre(dir)` blocks.\n- **Rationale**: safe by default, so a new command cannot forget to reconcile, and there is one code path for\n  every reconcile.\n- **Revisit when**: a command appears that must neither import nor reconcile.\n\n### Decision: Diff against the recorded configuration, not against previous file contents\n- **Alternatives**: remember what troupe wrote (hashes) and ignore it; diff against the previous snapshot.\n- **Selected**: fold the history into `path → (value, created_seq)` and diff the files' leaves against it.\n- **Rationale**: idempotent. It picks up of...[truncated 5989 chars]
assistant: ## Status\nThe design is written to `spec/spec/config-file-sync/design.md` and committed on `main` (`161879d`), with the requirements recorded as approved. The design itself is not approved yet.\n\n## Discovery Type\nLight discovery, since this extends existing code and adds no new dependency.\n\n## Key Findings\n- **Reconcile goes inside the step that opens a theatre for every command.** It's on by default, only `history` turns it off, and every `watch` check goes through the same step. It compares the files with what the history has recorded, not with the previous file contents. That catches edits made while nothing was running, and a `set`'s write-back finds nothing new because the `set` already recorded it.\n- **A lock is required.** I confirmed that two processes adding to the history at once collide. The design uses a lock on the `.troupe/` directory itself, which creates no file. It's held from just after the config file is found until the command ends. That closes the gap between a `set` writing its files and recording its events, during which `watch` could otherwise record the write-back again.\n- **`watch` checks files every 0.2 seconds.** It snapshots the watched files *before* each load and re-reads them by name, which makes the `watching` line a true promise and handles editors that save by renaming. Any failure is retried on the next check, with each distinct error message printed once. Signals only set a flag, so a reconcile in progress always finishes, and `watch` exits 0.\n\n## Review Gate\nThe design passed. All 44 requirement IDs are traced, every component has a file, and the boundary sections are filled in. One pass removed a stray component name, `StopFlag`, from the traceability table.\n\n## Research Log\n`research.md` now also records the design decisions, the alternatives considered, the synthesis notes and the risks.\n\n## Next Action\nReview the design, then:\n```\n/method-command config-file-sync\n```\nOptionally run `/method-command config-file-sync` first. `-y` approves the design and goes straight to tasks.

assistant: I stopped before generating tasks: the design is not approved yet.\n\n`spec/spec/config-file-sync/spec.json` shows `approvals.design.approved: false`, and no `-y` was passed. The project rules ask for a human review at each phase, so I didn't treat running this step as approval. The working tree is clean, and `design.md` is as committed in `161879d`.\n\nThese are the design decisions most worth checking before you approve:\n- **Reconcile inside the shared opening step:** `open_theatre` reconciles by default for every command and every `watch` check. Only `history` turns it off.\n- **Lock on `.troupe/`:** every command, `history` included, holds a lock on the `.troupe/` directory itself from just after the config file is found until it ends. It creates no file and waits as long as needed.\n- **Polling:** `watch` checks the files every 0.2 seconds and uses no file-watching library. Failures are retried on every check, and each distinct error message is printed once.\n- **Signals:** SIGINT and SIGTERM only set a flag. A reconcile in progress always finishes, then `watch` exits 0.\n- **Order of `remove` events:** within one reconcile, `remove` events follow the order in which those leaves were created in the history.\n- **Small changes to step-1 files:** `scalar_equal` moves into `troupe.config.scalars`. The shell keeps the raw `--theatre` text so `watch` can print `<dir>` as given. `run()` gets a streaming path so `watch` can print its line and keep running.\n\nTo approve the design and generate tasks, run:\n```\n/method-command config-file-sync -y\n```\nOr reply "approved" and I'll mark the design approved and generate the tasks; you'll still approve the task list separately. If you want changes, tell me which and I'll revise `design.md`.

assistant: I've generated the task plan at `spec/spec/config-file-sync/tasks.md` and committed it on `main` (`7629e92`). The design is recorded as approved.\n\n## Task Summary\nThere are 5 major tasks with 12 sub-tasks, each about 1–3 hours. All 44 requirements are mapped to at least one task.\n1. **Foundation (1.1):** the typed value comparison moves into core code, and the watch constants are added.\n2. **Core (2.1–2.3):** the recorded configuration (2.1) and the theatre lock (2.2) can run in parallel. Reconcile (2.3) follows.\n3. **Integration (3.1–3.3):**\n   - 3.1 makes every command take the lock and reconcile by default. In the same change, `history` opts out and the five step-1 tests that expect hand edits to add no events are rewritten, so no commit leaves `history` reconciling or the suite failing.\n   - 3.2 updates the docstrings, the step-1 spec notes and the roadmap.\n   - 3.3 adds the end-to-end tests through the real CLI.\n4. **Watch (4.1–4.3):** the watch loop, then the CLI streaming support (which can run alongside it), then the `watch` command together with the helper that runs `watch` in the background for tests.\n5. **Validation (5.1–5.3):** end-to-end `watch` tests run three times in a row under `pytest -n auto`, then the full quality gate and the traceability check.\n\n## Quality Validation\n- Every design component, step-1 file change and superseded test is covered by a task.\n- The task-plan review gate passed.\n- The task-graph sanity review was run independently by a separate reviewer. The first verdict was NEEDS_FIXES with 12 findings; after repairs, the second was **PASS**. I also applied its two remaining small points: signal handlers are restored in a `finally`, and a typo in `design.md` is fixed.\n\n## Next Action\nTasks generated. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_scalars.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"from troupe.config.scalars import ScalarValue, resolve_plain_scalar\",\"from troupe.config.scalars import ScalarValue, resolve_plain_scalar, scalar_equal\",1)\ns+='''\n\n# --- scalar_equal (typed comparison, config-file-sync) ----------------------------------------\n\nDIFFERENT_TYPES_OF_ONE: list[ScalarValue] = [1, 1.0, True, \"1\"]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"left\", DIFFERENT_TYPES_OF_ONE)\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"right\", DIFFERENT_TYPES_OF_ONE)\ndef test_core_scalar_equal_tells_one_one_point_zero_true_and_string_one_apart(\n    left: ScalarValue, right: ScalarValue\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.5: ``1``, ``1.0``, ``True`` and ``\"1\"`` are pairwise different; each equals itself.\"\"\"\n    assert scalar_equal(left, right) is (left is right)\n\n\ndef test_core_scalar_equal_treats_nan_as_equal_to_nan() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.5: floats compare by ``repr``, so ``nan`` equals ``nan`` and ``0.0`` differs from ``-0.0``.\"\"\"\n    assert scalar_equal(math.nan, float(\"nan\"))\n    assert not scalar_equal(0.0, -0.0)\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\n\np='tests/test_constants.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns+='''\n\ndef test_watch_constants_hold_the_poll_interval_and_the_readiness_line_prefix() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 5.1, 5.3: watch polls every 0.2 s and its readiness line starts with ``watching ``.\"\"\"\n    assert constants.WATCH_POLL_SECONDS == 0.2\n    assert constants.WATCHING_LINE_PREFIX == \"watching \"\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"^1\\. \\|^5\\.\\|Requirement 5\" spec/spec/config-file-sync/requirements.md | head; sed -n 140,146p spec/spec/config-file-sync/requirements.md; grep -n \"^### Requirement\" spec/spec/config-file-sync/requirements.md"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; sed -i 's/\"\"\"Req 5.1, 5.3: watch polls/\"\"\"Req 4.3, 5.1: watch polls/' tests/test_constants.py\ncat > tests/test_core_boundary.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Import boundary of the core configuration layer (config-file-sync, Allowed Dependencies).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport troupe.config\nfrom troupe.config import scalars\nfrom troupe.config.writeback import compare\n\nCONFIG_PACKAGE = Path(troupe.config.__file__).parent\nWRITEBACK_PACKAGE = \"troupe.config.writeback\"\n\n\ndef _imported_modules(source: Path) -> list[str]:\n    modules: list[str] = []\n    for node in ast.walk(ast.parse(source.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):\n            modules.extend(alias.name for alias in node.names)\n        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.module is not None:\n            modules.append(node.module)\n    return modules\n\n\ndef test_no_core_config_module_imports_the_writeback_package() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.5: core code compares values without depending on the write-back package.\"\"\"\n    core_modules = sorted(CONFIG_PACKAGE.glob(\"*.py\"))\n    assert core_modules\n\n    offenders = {\n        module.name: imported\n        for module in core_modules\n        for imported in _imported_modules(module)\n        if imported == WRITEBACK_PACKAGE or imported.startswith(WRITEBACK_PACKAGE + \".\")\n    }\n\n    assert offenders == {}\n\n\ndef test_writeback_compare_still_exports_the_core_scalar_equal() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.5: ``set`` and reconcile use one and the same typed comparison.\"\"\"\n    assert compare.scalar_equal is scalars.scalar_equal\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_scalars.py tests/test_constants.py tests/test_core_boundary.py 2>&1 | tail -25"}

docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport textwrap\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, ExitCode\nfrom troupe.config.lock import TheatreLock\n\nCONFIG = \"name: demo\\n\"\nCHILD_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 60.0\nHOLD_SECONDS = 0.5\n\n# The child first proves the lock is busy with a non-blocking attempt, then announces that it\n# is about to block, takes the lock through TheatreLock and reports the wall-clock time it got it.\nCHILD_SCRIPT = textwrap.dedent(\n    \"\"\"\n    import fcntl, os, sys, time\n    from pathlib import Path\n    from troupe.config.lock import TheatreLock\n\n    config_dir = Path(sys.argv[1])\n    fd = os.open(config_dir, os.O_RDONLY)\n    try:\n        fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB)\n        print(\"free\", flush=True)\n    except BlockingIOError:\n        print(\"busy\", flush=True)\n    finally:\n        os.close(fd)\n    print(\"waiting\", flush=True)\n    lock = TheatreLock.acquire(config_dir)\n    print(f\"acquired {time.time()!r}\", flush=True)\n    lock.release()\n    \"\"\"\n)\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef config_dir(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> Path:\n    return make_theatre(CONFIG) / CONFIG_DIR_NAME\n\n\ndef _listing(directory: Path) -> list[str]:\n    return sorted(str(path.relative_to(directory)) for path in directory.rglob(\"*\"))\n\n\ndef _read_line(process: subprocess.Popen[str]) -> str:\n    assert process.stdout is not None\n    line = process.stdout.readline()\n    assert line, f\"child exited early: {process.stderr.read() if process.stderr else ''}\"\n    return line.strip()\n\n\ndef test_child_process_waits_until_the_parent_releases(config_dir: Path) -> None:\...[truncated 5647 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The exclusive per-theatre lock (6.4, 7.1, 7.2).\n\nEvery command and every watch sync on a theatre holds this lock, so two reconciles, or a reconcile\nand a ``set``, never record their events interleaved (6.4). The lock is a blocking exclusive\n``flock`` on a read-only descriptor of the theatre's existing ``.troupe/`` directory itself, so no\nlock file is created (7.1, 7.2); the kernel releases it when the holding process exits.\n\nThe lock is not reentrant: acquiring a directory that this process already holds raises\n:class:`RuntimeError` (a programming error) instead of deadlocking.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport fcntl\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Self\n\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError\n\n# Real paths of the ``.troupe/`` directories this process currently holds.\n_held: set[Path] = set()\n\n\nclass TheatreLock:\n    \"\"\"A held exclusive lock on one theatre's ``.troupe/`` directory.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, config_dir: Path, real_path: Path, fd: int) -> None:\n        self.config_dir = config_dir\n        self._real_path = real_path\n        self._fd: int | None = fd\n\n    @classmethod\n    def acquire(cls, config_dir: Path) -> Self:\n        \"\"\"Block until the lock on ``config_dir`` is held and return it.\n\n        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` when this process already holds it, and\n        :class:`ConfigLoadError` naming ``config_dir`` when it cannot be opened or locked.\n        \"\"\"\n        real_path = config_dir.resolve()\n        if real_path in _held:\n            raise RuntimeError(f\"{config_dir} is already locked by this process\")\n        try:\n            fd = os.open(config_dir, os.O_RDONLY)\n        except OSError as error:\n            raise ConfigLoadError(f\"cannot lock: {error.strerror}\", file=config_dir) from error\n        try:\n            fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n        except OSError as error:\n            os.close(fd)\n            raise ConfigLoadError(f\"cannot...[truncated 531 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/lock.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''        try:\n            fd = os.open(config_dir, os.O_RDONLY)\n        except OSError as error:\n            raise ConfigLoadError(f\"cannot lock: {error.strerror}\", file=config_dir) from error\n        try:\n            fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n        except OSError as error:\n            os.close(fd)\n            raise ConfigLoadError(f\"cannot lock: {error.strerror}\", file=config_dir) from error\n''','''        try:\n            fd = os.open(config_dir, os.O_RDONLY)\n        except OSError as error:\n            raise _lock_error(config_dir, error) from error\n        try:\n            fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n        except OSError as error:\n            os.close(fd)\n            raise _lock_error(config_dir, error) from error\n''')\ns+='''\n\ndef _lock_error(config_dir: Path, error: OSError) -> ConfigLoadError:\n    reason = error.strerror or type(error).__name__\n    return ConfigLoadError(f\"cannot lock the theatre: {reason}\", file=config_dir)\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format src/troupe/config/lock.py tests/test_lock.py; uv run pytest -n auto -q 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run mypy --strict . 2>&1 | tail -2; uv run ruff check .; uv run ruff format --check . | tail -1"}

The lock\nis a flock on the existing .troupe/ directory, so it creates no file (decision E) and\nthe kernel frees it when a process dies. A nested acquire raises instead of deadlocking.\" && git log --oneline -1"}

recorded_leaves\nfrom troupe.config.reconcile import reconcile, reconcile_changes\nfrom troupe.config.resolve import flatten_leaves\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import ScalarValue, scalar_equal\nfrom relative).write_text(content, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    def remove(self, relative: str) -> None:\n        (self.directory / CONFIG_DIR_NAME / relative).unlink()\n\n    def load(self) -> ConfigDocument:\n        return load_config(find_config_file(self.directory))\n\n    def changes(self) -> tuple[LeafChange, ...]:\n        \"\"\"The pure diff of the files on disk against the recorded configuration.\"\"\"\n        return reconcile_changes(self.load(), recorded_leaves(self.store.records()))\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef open_theatre(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Reconcile: the files against the recorded configuration (2.1-2.8, 3.1-3.3, 5.5).\n\n:func:`reconcile_changes` is pure. Every leaf of the loaded files, in document order, that is\nmissing from the recorded configuration or whose recorded value differs under the typed comparison\n(:func:`~troupe.config.scalars.scalar_equal`) gives a :class:`SetChange`; then every recorded leaf\nmissing from the files, in creation-``seq`` order, gives a :class:`RemoveChange`. Formatting,\ncomments, quoting, key order and whether a value is written in place or through an include never\nchange a leaf, so they give no change (2.4).\n\n:func:`reconcile` records those changes with the store's one-transaction append, so a reconcile is\nrecorded all or nothing (2.7). It never reads or writes user files.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Sequence\n\nfrom troupe.config.history import LeafChange, RemoveChange, SetChange\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigDocument\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.recorded import RecordedLeaf, recorded_leaves\nfrom troupe.config.resolve import flatten_leaves\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import scalar_equal\nfrom troupe.config.store import HistoryStore\n\n\ndef reconcile_changes(\n    document: ConfigDocument, recorded: Sequence[RecordedLeaf]\n) -> tuple[LeafChange, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Return the changes that make ``recorded`` equal to the leaves of ``document``.\n\n    ``recorded`` must be ordered by ``created_seq`` (as :func:`recorded_leaves` returns it): sets\n    come in the files' document order, then removes in that order (2.6).\n    \"\"\"\n    recorded_by_path = {leaf.path: leaf for leaf in recorded}\n    changes: list[LeafChange] = []\n    file_paths: set[str] = set()\n    for leaf in flatten_leaves(document.root):\n        path = str(leaf.path)\n        file_paths.add(path)\n        current = recorded_by_path.get(path)\n        if current is None:\n            changes.append(SetChange(path=leaf.path, ...[truncated 1060 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/reconcile.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''            changes.append(RemoveChange(path=ConfigPath.parse(gone.path), old=gone.value))\n    return tuple(changes)\n''','''            changes.append(RemoveChange(path=_stored_path(gone.path), old=gone.value))\n    return tuple(changes)\n\n\ndef _stored_path(text: str) -> ConfigPath:\n    \"\"\"Rebuild the path of a recorded leaf from its stored dotted text, so ``str`` round-trips.\n\n    Unlike :meth:`ConfigPath.parse` (which validates a user-given PATH), this accepts any stored\n    text, because a leaf may sit under a key that is empty or contains the separator, and removing\n    such a leaf must not fail with a usage error.\n    \"\"\"\n    return ConfigPath(tuple(text.split(PATH_SEPARATOR)))\n''')\ns=s.replace('from collections.abc import Sequence\\n','from collections.abc import Sequence\\n\\nfrom troupe.config.constants import PATH_SEPARATOR\\n')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='tests/test_reconcile.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns+='''\n\ndef test_removing_leaves_under_unusual_keys_round_trips_their_paths(\n    open_theatre: MakeTheatreWithStore,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.3: leaves under an empty key or a key with the separator are removed by their path.\"\"\"\n    theatre = open_theatre('\"\": 1\\\\n\"a.\": 2\\\\nb: 3\\\\n')\n    theatre.write(\"troupe.yml\", \"b: 3\\\\n\")\n\n    changes = reconcile(theatre.load(), theatre.store)\n\n    assert [(str(change.path), change.old) for change in changes] == [(\"\", 1), (\"a.\", 2)]\n    assert all(isinstance(change, RemoveChange) for change in changes)\n    _assert_recorded_equals_files(theatre)\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format src/troupe/config/reconcile.py tests/test_reconcile.py; uv run pytest -n auto -q 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run mypy --strict . 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run ruff check . ; uv run ruff format --check . | tail -2"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/theatre.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''\"\"\"The theatre session: use cases shared by every subcommand (6.1-6.9, 7.4, 7.9, 8.3-8.5).\n\nA command opens its theatre with ``with open_theatre(directory) as theatre:``. Opening follows a\nfixed order: locate the configuration file, load it from disk, open the history store, import the\nconfiguration once. Nothing is created when locating or loading fails (7.4). Leaving the ``with``\nblock closes the history store, whether the block succeeded or failed (7.9).\n\nFiles are the truth (product-owner decision A): ``theatre.document`` is the configuration as read\nfrom disk by this command, :func:`show_value` reads only that document, and the history is change\nhistory only. A hand edit made since the import is shown and never recorded (6.9, 7.3).\n\"\"\"''','''\"\"\"The theatre session: use cases shared by every subcommand (6.1-6.9, 7.4, 7.9, 8.3-8.5).\n\nA command opens its theatre with ``with open_theatre(directory) as theatre:``. Opening follows a\nfixed order: locate the configuration file, take the theatre lock on its directory, load it from\ndisk, open the history store, then import the configuration when it was never imported, or\notherwise reconcile the history with the files unless the caller opted out (config-file-sync 1.1,\n1.3, 1.4). Nothing is created when locating or loading fails (7.4), and a load failure records\nnothing and leaves the lock free (config-file-sync 1.5). Leaving the ``with`` block closes the\nhistory store and then releases the lock, whether the block succeeded or failed (7.9;\nconfig-file-sync 7.3). The lock is held from load to close, so the events of two commands on one\ntheatre never interleave (config-file-sync 1.7, 6.1-6.4).\n\nFiles are the truth (product-owner decision A): ``theatre.document`` is the configuration as read\nfrom disk by this command, and :func:`show_value` reads only that document. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Reconcile-first, end to end through the real ``troupe config`` console script.\n\nEvery command except ``history`` reconciles an imported theatre with its files before doing its own\nwork (config-file-sync 1.1); ``history`` never reconciles (1.3), so it is the safe observer used\nhere. Each test builds a real theatre on disk, hand-edits its files between commands, and asserts the\nexact stdout, stderr and exit code of each command and the exact ``history`` lines. The key cases run\non both ``troupe.yml`` and ``troupe.yaml`` theatres.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import run_config, run_set, snapshot_tree\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre\n\ntype Event = tuple[int, str, str, Any, Any]\n\"\"\"One history line as ``(seq, op, path, old, new)``.\"\"\"\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(params=[\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"])\ndef config_name(request: pytest.FixtureRequest) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The root config file name of the theatre (both lookups are covered).\"\"\"\n    name: str = request.param\n    return name\n\n\ndef _root(theatre: Path, config_name: str) -> Path:\n    return theatre / \".troupe\" / config_name\n\n\ndef _history_bytes(theatre: Path) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"The exact ``troupe config history`` stdout (asserting success and an empty stderr).\"\"\"\n    result = run_config(\"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stderr) == (0, \"\"), result.stderr\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef _events(theatre: Path) -> list[Event]:\n    \"\"\"Every recorded event, parsed from the ``history`` lines.\"\"\"\n    rows = [json.loads(line) for line in _history_bytes(theatre).decode(\"utf-8\").splitlines()]\n    return [(row[\"seq\"], row[\"op\"], row[\"path\"], row[\"old\"], row[\"new\"]) for row in rows]\n\n\ndef _show(theatre: Path,...[truncated 13081 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The watcher loop in-process on real theatres (config-file-sync 3.4, 3.5, 4.2, 4.4, 4.8, 4.9, 5.1-5.7).\n\nEach test builds a real theatre on disk, starts a :class:`Watcher` in the test thread (``start``)\nand runs its loop in a background thread with a :class:`threading.Event` as the stop flag. The\nwatcher is always stopped and joined in teardown.\n\nThe history is observed the way ``history`` observes it, without reconciling: the test takes the\ntheatre's ``flock`` on its own descriptor of ``.troupe/`` (so it waits for a sync in progress) and\nreads the store directly. It does not use ``open_theatre``, whose in-process guard refuses a second\nholder of the same theatre. Positive timing assertions poll with a 2 s deadline measured from the\nedit, no more often than every 100 ms; negative assertions wait 2.5 s and then check.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport fcntl\nimport os\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Iterator\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config import watch\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME, HISTORY_STORE_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError\nfrom troupe.config.history import HistoryRecord\nfrom troupe.config.store import HistoryStore\nfrom troupe.config.theatre import Theatre, open_theatre\nfrom troupe.config.watch import Watcher\n\nHAPPENS_SECONDS = 2.0  # 5.1, 5.3: reconciled within 2 s of the change\nNOTHING_SECONDS = 2.5  # negative assertions wait this long, then check\nHISTORY_POLL_SECONDS = 0.1  # never poll the history more often (each poll takes the lock)\nJOIN_SECONDS = 10.0\n\n\nclass RunningWatcher:\n    \"\"\"A started watcher whose loop runs in a background thread.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, watcher: Watcher, stop: threading.Event, reports: list[str]) -> None:\n        s...[truncated 22618 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The watcher loop behind ``troupe config watch`` (config-file-sync 3.4, 3.5, 4.2, 4.4, 4.9, 5.1-5.7).\n\nThe watcher observes the theatre by polling byte snapshots, without any file-watching dependency:\n\n- The **watch set** is the display path of every file the last successful load read (the root\n  configuration file and every YAML or text include, at any depth), plus both root candidates\n  ``<theatre>/.troupe/troupe.yml`` and ``troupe.yaml``, so an added or renamed root is seen (5.2).\n  Files outside the watch set are never read (5.6).\n- A **snapshot** maps each watched path to its bytes, read by path so that a replace-by-rename is\n  seen (5.2), or to ``None`` when the file is missing or unreadable. A touch leaves the bytes\n  unchanged and is therefore not a change (5.7).\n- A **sync** repeats \"snapshot the watch set, open the theatre (which imports or reconciles), then\n  recompute the watch set from the loaded files\" until the watch set is stable, and keeps that\n  snapshot as the baseline. The snapshot is taken *before* the load it describes, so any write made\n  after it differs from the baseline at the next tick and nothing is missed (4.4, 5.1). A newly\n  included file enlarges the watch set and forces one more round that puts it under the baseline\n  (3.4); a removed include's file leaves the watch set and is never read again (3.5).\n- The **loop** syncs whenever the snapshot differs from the baseline or a retry is pending. A\n  :class:`TroupeError` (a load failure, 5.3, or a history store failure, 5.4) is reported once per\n  distinct message and retried on every tick; nothing is recorded until a sync succeeds, because\n  ``open_theatre`` records only after a successful load, in one transaction. Between ticks the loop\n  sleeps in short steps that check the stop flag.\n\nThe watcher only reads user files; it never creates, modifies, renames or deletes one (4.9).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport time\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Iterab...[truncated 3755 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_watch_loop.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''def _write(path: Path, text: str) -> None:\n    path.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n''','''def _write(path: Path, text: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Save ``text`` atomically (new file renamed over the old), so the watcher never reads a\n    half-written file; in-place rewrites are covered separately with :func:`_write_in_place`.\"\"\"\n    temporary = path.with_name(f\".{path.name}.test-tmp\")\n    temporary.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    os.replace(temporary, path)\n\n\ndef _write_in_place(path: Path, text: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Rewrite the existing file in place (truncate, then write).\"\"\"\n    path.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n''')\ns=s.replace('''def test_edit_made_between_start_and_run_is_recorded(''','''def test_in_place_rewrite_is_recorded_within_two_seconds(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 5.1, 5.2: a file rewritten in place is reconciled within 2 s of the change.\n\n    A sync may race the truncate-then-write and see an intermediate state, so only the final\n    recorded value is asserted (5.5 allows recording intermediate states).\n    \"\"\"\n    theatre_dir = _imported(make_theatre, \"a: 1\\\\n\")\n    config = _config_dir(theatre_dir) / \"troupe.yml\"\n    inode = config.stat().st_ino\n    with running_watcher(theatre_dir):\n        edited = time.monotonic()\n        _write_in_place(config, \"a: 2\\\\n\")\n\n        _wait_until(\n            lambda: _ops(_records(theatre_dir))[-1][1:] in {(\"a\", 1, 2), (\"a\", None, 2)},\n            since=edited,\n            what=\"a recorded as 2\",\n        )\n    assert config.stat().st_ino == inode  # really rewritten in place\n\n\ndef test_edit_made_between_start_and_run_is_recorded(''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"_write(config, \\\"a: \\[\\|write_bytes\" tests/test_watch_loop.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/aba_demo.py <<'EOF'\nimport tempfile, threading, time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config import watch\nfrom troupe.config.theatre import open_theatre\nfrom troupe.config.watch import Watcher\n\nd = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\".troupe\").mkdir()\ncfg = d/\".troupe\"/\"troupe.yml\"; cfg.write_text(\"a: 1\\nb: 2\\n\")\nwith open_theatre(d): pass\nreal = open_theatre\nstate = {\"armed\": False}\ndef racing_open(directory, *, reconcile=True):\n    # an in-place save of the SAME content races the load: the load sees the truncated file\n    if state[\"armed\"]:\n        state[\"armed\"] = False\n        cfg.write_bytes(b\"\")\n        t = real(directory, reconcile=reconcile)  # loads + reconciles the empty file\n        cfg.write_bytes(b\"a: 1\\nb: 2\\n\")          # write completes, same bytes as before\n        return t\n    return real(directory, reconcile=reconcile)\nwatch.open_theatre = racing_open\nstop = threading.Event()\nw = Watcher(d, report=print, stop_requested=stop.is_set); w.start()\nth = threading.Thread(target=w.run); th.start()\nstate[\"armed\"] = True\nw.pending = True  # trigger one sync (stands in for any change that starts a sync)\ntime.sleep(3); stop.set(); th.join()\nwatch.open_theatre = real\nprint(\"file:\", cfg.read_text().split())\nimport subprocess\nprint(subprocess.run([\"uv\",\"run\",\"troupe\",\"config\",\"history\",\"--theatre\",str(d)],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/aba_demo.py"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "Review of task 4.1 came back REJECTED (round 1). …", "message": "Review of task 4.1 came back REJECTED (round 1). Keep the same task context (task 4.1, Boundary: Watcher, the same validation commands) and fix this:\n\nFINDING (Important, correctness — Req 5.5, 5.7, 4.4; design \"Watch lifecycle > Sync\"): Watcher._sync keeps the snapshot taken before the load as the baseline, not the bytes the load actually read. If a file goes X → transient → X while the load runs (an in-place save of unchanged content, or the second of two quick in-place saves), the load reads e.g. an empty file. An empty file is valid YAML that loads as an empty configuration, so reconcile records a `remove` for every leaf. The write then finishes with bytes equal to the baseline, the next snapshot equals the baseline, and the watcher never syncs again: history holds phantom removes that are never repaired. The reviewer reproduced this deterministically by patching watch.open_theatre to truncate the config before the real load and restore the identical bytes after it. After 3 s of run(), the file was `a: 1, b: 2` but history was import a, import b, remove a, remove b.\n\nREMEDIATION:\n- In Watcher._sync, build the baseline from what the load actually read: for every source in theatre.document.files, set baseline[source.path] = source.text.encode(\"utf-8\"). First check in src/troupe/config/loader.py that the decode is strict UTF-8 with no newline translation and keeps the BOM, so that this round-trips exactly; if it doesn't round-trip, use another round when any loaded text differs from its snapshot entry instead.\n- Keep the snapshot value only for watch-set paths the load did not read (for example the absent root candidate).\n- Keep the readiness property: anything changed after the snapshot must still be detected.\n- Add a deterministic regression test to tests/test_watch_loop.py using the same technique: patch watch.open_theatre to empty the config, call the real opener, then res...[truncated 747 chars]

assistant: I fixed the round-1 finding, where a sync that loaded a half-written file could leave phantom removes in history. The watcher's baseline is now the bytes the load actually read, not the snapshot taken before the load. A new regression test failed before the fix, fails again when the fix is reverted, and passes with it. The full gate passes.\n\n- **The fix:** in `Watcher._sync`, the baseline is now `snapshot | _loaded_bytes(document)`. Every file the load read gets the exact bytes it read, re-encoded from `source.text`. That round-trips exactly because `loader._decode` is strict UTF-8 with no newline translation and keeps the BOM. Paths the load did not read, such as the absent root candidate, keep their snapshot value.\n- **Readiness still holds:**\n  - A write after the snapshot that the load did not see differs from the baseline at the next tick.\n  - A write the load did see is already in what was recorded.\n  - A transient state the load read, such as an empty file mid-save, no longer matches the file once it returns to its earlier bytes, so the watcher syncs again and repairs the history.\n- **New tests in `tests/test_watch_loop.py`:**\n  - `test_transient_content_read_by_a_sync_is_repaired_when_the_file_returns` uses the reviewer's technique and checks that the recorded leaves equal the files within 2 s.\n  - `test_in_place_rewrite_with_unchanged_content_leaves_the_record_equal_to_the_files` does 50 quick in-place rewrites with unchanged content.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.1\n- TASK_BRIEF: Watcher(directory, report, stop_requested, poll_seconds). The watch set is the display path of every loaded file plus both root names. Snapshots hold bytes, or None when missing or unreadable. A sync repeats snapshot → open_theatre → recompute the watch set until it is stable; the baseline is then the bytes the load read for loaded files and the pre-load snapshot for the other watched paths. start() does the first sync and raises TroupeError. run() syncs when the snapshot differs or a retry is pending, reports each distinct error message once, keeps a retry pending, never raises TroupeError, sleeps in stop-checking steps, and never writes user files. Round-1 fix: a transient state read by a load is repaired once the file settles.\n- FILES_CHANGED: src/troupe/config/watch.py (new), tests/test_watch_loop.py (new)\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 3.4, 3.5, 4.2, 4.4, 4.6 (stop via flag), 4.8, 4.9, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3, 5.4, 5.5, 5.6, 5.7; Boundary Context "Choices made where the contract is silent"\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Components and Interfaces > Core > Watcher (config/watch.py); System Flows > Watch lifecycle (Sync/Retry/Stop); Error Handling > Error Strategy; Testing Strategy > Integration Tests; research.md decisions "Observe by polling byte snapshots" and "Failures while watching are retried every tick and reported once per distinct message"\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT:\n  - Round 1, before the fix, with the regression test added: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_watch_loop.py -k "transient or unchanged_content"` gave `E  Failed: not within 2.0 s: the recorded configuration equal to the files` / `FAILED tests/test_watch_loop.py::test_transient_content_read_by_a_sync_is_repaired_when_the_file_returns` / `1 failed, 1 passed`. After the fix, temporarily restoring `self.baseline = snapshot` made it fail again (`1 failed`); the fix is restored.\n  - The unchanged-content test passed even before the fix, because whether it hits the bug depends on timing. It is the coverage test the coordinator asked for, not the RED proof.\n  - Original RED: `ImportError: cannot import name 'watch' from 'troupe.config'` before the module existed.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/test_watch_loop.py` ×3 → 26 passed each time (~8 s); `uv run pytest -n auto -q` → 2040 passed in 73.65s; `uv run mypy --strict .` → Success: no issues found in 96 source files; `uv run ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `uv run ruff format --check .` → 97 files already formatted\n- CONCERNS: (1) Each change can now cost one extra no-op sync. If the file moves on between the load and the end of the sync, the pre-load snapshot is no longer the baseline, so the next tick syncs again, and that sync finds no leaf difference and records nothing. (2) The retry-pending flag still cannot be tested on its own, because a failed sync leaves the baseline out of date, so the watcher retries anyway. It is kept because the design asks for it. (3) Some tests wrap `watch.open_theatre` to count syncs or to change files mid-sync. (4) The stop test requires stop to return within 0.2 s (`WATCH_POLL_SECONDS`) while a 3 s poll interval is running. (5) The unreadable-store test skips when run as root.\n- EVIDENCE: `Watcher._sync` builds the baseline as `snapshot | _loaded_bytes(document)`, and `_loaded_bytes` re-encodes `source.text` for each file in `document.files` (src/troupe/config/watch.py). The new tests are `test_transient_content_read_by_a_sync_is_repaired_when_the_file_returns` (deterministic: it truncates, loads, then restores the same bytes, and requires the live leaves to equal the files within 2 s) and `test_in_place_rewrite_with_unchanged_content_leaves_the_record_equal_to_the_files`. The 24 tests from the first round still pass, including `test_edit_made_while_a_sync_is_loading_is_recorded_at_the_next_tick`.

assistant: ## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 4.1\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -n auto -q`: 2040 passed, exit 0; `tests/test_watch_loop.py` passed 26 tests in each of 3 runs under `-n auto`)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (`mypy --strict .` found no issues; `ruff check .` passed; `ruff format --check .` reported 97 files already formatted)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (only the untracked `src/troupe/config/watch.py` and `tests/test_watch_loop.py`)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN\n  - RED phase: VERIFIED (ImportError for the new module; the round-1 regression test fails when the old baseline is put back, see finding 3)\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. **The round-1 finding is fixed.** `Watcher._sync` now sets `self.baseline = snapshot | _loaded_bytes(document)`. For every file the load read, the baseline is the bytes it actually read. The pre-load snapshot is kept only for watch-set paths the load did not read, such as the absent root candidate.\n     - The keys line up: both the baseline keys and the watch set come from `source.path`.\n     - I re-ran `/tmp/aba_demo.py`. The transient `remove a` / `remove b` events are now followed by `set a` / `set b`, so the record matches the files again without anything else happening.\n  2. **Readiness still holds, and is slightly stronger.**\n     - A write that lands between the snapshot and the load is read by the load and therefore recorded.\n     - A write after the load read the file differs from the loaded bytes and triggers a sync at the next tick.\n     - The loop still repeats until the watch set is stable, so a newly added include gets a baseline entry.\n     - If loaded text ever differed from the file for some other reason, the worst outcome is one extra sync, never a missed change.\n  3. **The new tests are sound.**\n     - `test_transient_content_read_by_a_sync_is_repaired_when_the_file_returns` is deterministic: it patches `watch.open_theatre` to delegate to the real opener, and pytest's monkeypatch fixture restores it afterwards. I mutated a copy of the repo in `/tmp/mut` back to `self.baseline = snapshot`, and this test failed there.\n     - `test_in_place_rewrite_with_unchanged_content_leaves_the_record_equal_to_the_files` exercises the real race, but only probabilistically. Its final-state assertion is robust whether or not the race actually happens.\n  4. **The round-trip claim is correct.** In `src/troupe/config/loader.py`, `_decode` uses `data.decode("utf-8", errors="strict")` on the raw `read_bytes()` output. Python's UTF-8 codec keeps a BOM as U+FEFF and does no newline translation, so `text.encode("utf-8")` gives back the exact bytes. Override sources are used only for write-back and cost at most one extra sync.\n  5. **FYI.** These parts are still sound from round 1: no `TroupeError` escapes `run()`, reports are de-duplicated and reset after a success, stop is responsive, user files are never written, and the store is opened and closed per sync.\n- REMEDIATION: None required.\n- SUMMARY: The baseline is now the bytes each load actually read, which closes the X → transient → X lost-update race without weakening the readiness guarantee; a deterministic regression test that fails under the old baseline covers it, and every validation gate passes, including 3 stable runs of the module under `-n auto`.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Test support for ``troupe config watch``: the real command as a background subprocess.\n\n:class:`WatchProcess` starts ``troupe config watch --theatre DIR`` with the real console script\n(``support.troupe_executable``), reads its stdout and stderr on background threads (so a full pipe\nnever blocks the child and a wait never blocks the test), waits for the readiness line, polls\n``troupe config history`` (which never reconciles, so it is a safe observer) against a deadline,\nsends signals, and always terminates and kills the child when it is closed. Signals are only ever\nsent to the child: no test installs a signal handler in the pytest process for this.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport signal\nimport subprocess\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Sequence\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom types import TracebackType\nfrom typing import IO, Any, Self\n\nfrom support import invoke_troupe, troupe_executable\n\nREADY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0\n\"\"\"Longest wait for the ``watching`` line after the process starts.\"\"\"\n\nHISTORY_POLL_SECONDS = 0.1\n\"\"\"Shortest interval between two ``history`` polls (each poll briefly takes the theatre lock).\"\"\"\n\nEXIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0\n\"\"\"Longest wait for the child to exit after a signal or in teardown before it is killed.\"\"\"\n\nHistoryEvent = dict[str, Any]\n\"\"\"One parsed JSON line of ``troupe config history``.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass _PipeReader:\n    \"\"\"Collect everything a child writes to one pipe, on a daemon thread.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, pipe: IO[bytes]) -> None:\n        self._pipe = pipe\n        self._data = bytearray()\n        self._closed = False\n        self._changed = threading.Condition()\n        self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._pump, daemon=True)\n        self._thread.start()\n\n    def _pump(self) -> None:\n        try:\n            while chunk := self._pipe.read1(4096) if hasattr(self._pipe, \"read1\") else b\"\":\n               ...[truncated 7336 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"CLI ``troupe config watch``: registration and streaming handler (config-file-sync 2.7, 4.1-4.8).\n\nImports only :mod:`troupe.cli.shell` from the CLI package (never ``troupe.cli.main``, which imports\nthis module).\n\nSignals only request a stop: the SIGINT and SIGTERM handlers set a flag and never raise, so a\nreconcile in progress always finishes its single transaction (2.7), the theatre is closed and the\nlock released, and the command exits 0 without a traceback (4.6). The previous handlers are\nrestored in a ``finally`` on every exit path, including a start-up failure.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport signal\nfrom types import FrameType\nfrom typing import BinaryIO, TextIO\n\nfrom troupe.cli.shell import (\n    StreamHandler,\n    SubcommandRegistrar,\n    TroupeArgumentParser,\n    theatre_option_parent,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.constants import WATCHING_LINE_PREFIX\nfrom troupe.config.watch import Watcher\n\n_STOP_SIGNALS = (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM)\n\n\ndef handle_watch(args: argparse.Namespace, stdout: BinaryIO, stderr: TextIO) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Handler of ``troupe config watch``: sync, print ``watching <dir>``, follow until stopped.\n\n    A :class:`~troupe.config.errors.TroupeError` from the first sync propagates to ``run`` before\n    anything is written to stdout (4.8). The readiness line is the only thing ever written to\n    stdout (4.3, 4.5); every message while watching goes to stderr. Returns 0 after a stop (4.6).\n    \"\"\"\n    stop = False\n\n    def request_stop(signum: int, frame: FrameType | None) -> None:\n        nonlocal stop\n        stop = True\n\n    def report(message: str) -> None:\n        stderr.write(f\"{message}\\n\")\n        stderr.flush()\n\n    previous = {sig: signal.getsignal(sig) for sig in _STOP_SIGNALS}\n    try:\n        for sig in _STOP_SIGNALS:\n            signal.signal(sig, request_stop)\n        watcher = Watcher(args.theatre, report=report, stop_requested=lambda: stop)\n        watcher.st...[truncated 1282 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config watch``: the readiness line, stopping on a signal and start-up failures.\n\nEvery test drives the real console script in a subprocess through ``watch_support.WatchProcess``;\nsignals are only ever sent to that child. The one in-process test calls ``run`` for a start-up\nfailure, which returns before any signal could matter, and checks that the previous SIGINT and\nSIGTERM handlers of the pytest process are back afterwards.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport io\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import write_theatre\nfrom troupe.cli.main import run\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME, HISTORY_STORE_NAME\nfrom watch_support import WatchProcess\n\nCONFIG = \"name: demo\\nretries: 1\\n\"\n\n\ndef _theatre(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    return write_theatre(tmp_path / \"theatre\", CONFIG)\n\n\ndef _env_without_theatre() -> dict[str, str]:\n    return {key: value for key, value in os.environ.items() if key != \"TROUPE_THEATRE\"}\n\n\n# ---- 4.3, 4.5, 4.6: the readiness line and stopping ----\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"given\", [\"theatre/\", \"./theatre\"], ids=[\"trailing-slash\", \"dot-relative\"])\ndef test_readiness_line_echoes_the_theatre_directory_as_given(tmp_path: Path, given: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.3, 4.5: stdout is exactly ``watching <dir>\\\\n`` with ``<dir>`` as typed.\"\"\"\n    _theatre(tmp_path)\n\n    with WatchProcess(given, cwd=tmp_path) as watch:\n        assert watch.wait_ready() == f\"watching {given}\\n\".encode()\n        assert watch.stop(signal.SIGINT) == 0\n\n    assert watch.stdout == f\"watching {given}\\n\".encode()\n\n\ndef test_readiness_line_names_the_current_directory_by_default(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.1, 4.3: without ``--theatre`` the theatre is the current directory, printed as such.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(tmp_path)\n    expected = f\"watching {theatre.resolve()}\\n\".encode()\n\n    with WatchProcess(Non...[truncated 4392 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config watch`` observing hand edits, end to end through the real console script.\n\nEvery test starts the real command in a subprocess through ``watch_support.WatchProcess`` and\nasserts the exact ``troupe config history`` records (``history`` never reconciles, so it is a safe\nobserver). A positive assertion polls ``history`` (at most every 100 ms) against a 2 s deadline\nmeasured from the edit; a negative assertion waits 2.5 s and then checks that nothing was recorded.\nEvery test ends by stopping ``watch`` with SIGTERM and checking exit 0, stdout exactly the one\nreadiness line, and an empty stderr (4.5, 4.6).\n\nMost edits are written atomically (a temporary file renamed over the target) so that ``watch``\nnever sees a half-written file; quick successive states may be reconciled together (5.5), and\nthese tests assert only the final recorded configuration. One root-file edit is an ordinary\nin-place write.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport time\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import write_theatre\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME\nfrom watch_support import HistoryEvent, WatchProcess\n\nNEGATIVE_WAIT_SECONDS = 2.5\n\"\"\"How long a test waits before asserting that nothing was recorded.\"\"\"\n\nROOT = \"# root of the demo theatre\\nname: demo\\nagents: file:agents.yml\\nprompt: file:prompt.txt\\n\"\nAGENTS = \"lead:\\n  model: opus\\n  retries: 3\\nhelpers: file:sub/helpers.yml\\n\"\nHELPERS = \"count: 1\\n\"\nPROMPT = \"You are helpful.\\n\"\n\nIMPORTS: list[HistoryEvent] = [\n    {\"seq\": 1, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"name\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"demo\"},\n    {\"seq\": 2, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"agents.lead.model\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"opus\"},\n    {\"seq\": 3, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"agents.lead.retries\", \"old\": None, \"new\": 3},\n    {\"seq\": 4, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"agents.helpers.count\",...[truncated 14022 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_e2e_watch.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index(\"# ---- 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 5.1, 5.2: the line comes first\"):s.index(\"# ---- 5.1, 2.8: edits to the root\")]\nnew='''# ---- 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 5.1, 5.2: an edit made the moment the line appears is recorded ----\n\n\ndef test_in_place_edit_made_the_moment_the_line_appears_is_recorded(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 5.1, 5.2: no edit is made before the line ``watching <dir>`` appears;\n    an ordinary in-place rewrite of the root made immediately after it (nothing in between) is\n    recorded within 2 s, so the history is exactly the start-up import followed by that one\n    ``set``; stdout stays exactly the one line until SIGTERM.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(tmp_path)\n    config = _config(theatre)\n\n    with WatchProcess(str(theatre), cwd=tmp_path) as watch:\n        assert watch.wait_ready() == f\"watching {theatre}\\\\n\".encode()\n        since = time.monotonic()\n        config.write_text(ROOT.replace(\"name: demo\", \"name: prod\"), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        expected = [*IMPORTS, _set(6, \"name\", \"demo\", \"prod\")]\n        events = watch.wait_for_history(lambda events: len(events) >= len(expected), since=since)\n        assert events == expected\n        _stop_cleanly(watch)\n\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nsed -n 25,60p src/troupe/cli/watch_command.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_e2e_watch.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nanchor=\"# ---- 5.2: replace by rename\"\nnew='''# ---- 5.1: the 2 s bound holds wherever in the watcher's poll cycle the edit falls ----\n\nPHASE_BANDS = 5\n\"\"\"Sessions the phase sweep is split into (so that pytest-xdist can run them side by side).\"\"\"\n\nPHASE_STEP_SECONDS = 0.1\n\"\"\"Spacing of the idle times the sweep covers; together they span 0 to 2.4 s.\"\"\"\n\nEDITS_PER_BAND = 5\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"band\", range(PHASE_BANDS))\ndef test_edit_after_any_idle_time_is_recorded_within_2_seconds(tmp_path: Path, band: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 5.1: after each record, ``watch`` is left idle for a different time before the next\n    edit, so that the edits fall at different points of its poll cycle (the five bands together\n    cover idle times 0 to 2.4 s in 0.1 s steps); every edit is still recorded within 2 s of the\n    edit, as exactly one ``set``. A watcher that checks the files less often than about every\n    2 s misses this bound for the edits that fall just after one of its checks.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(tmp_path)\n    config = _config(theatre)\n    idle_times = [\n        PHASE_STEP_SECONDS * (band + PHASE_BANDS * step) for step in range(EDITS_PER_BAND)\n    ]\n\n    with WatchProcess(str(theatre), cwd=tmp_path) as watch:\n        before = _started(watch, IMPORTS)\n        previous = \"demo\"\n        for number, idle in enumerate(idle_times, start=1):\n            time.sleep(idle)\n            value = f\"v{number}\"\n            new = _recorded_within_2_seconds(\n                watch,\n                lambda value=value: _write_atomically(\n                    config, ROOT.replace(\"name: demo\", f\"name: {value}\")\n                ),\n                before,\n            )\n            assert new == [_set(len(before) + 1, \"name\", previous, value)], f\"idle {idle:.1f} s\"\n            before += new\n            previous = value\n   ...[truncated 322 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_e2e_watch.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index(\"            new = _recorded_within_2_seconds(\\n                watch,\\n                lambda value=value\"):s.index(\"            assert new == [_set(len(before) + 1\")]\nnew='''            content = ROOT.replace(\"name: demo\", f\"name: {value}\")\n            new = _recorded_within_2_seconds(\n                watch, functools.partial(_write_atomically, config, content), before\n            )\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new).replace(\"import os\\n\",\"import functools\\nimport os\\n\",1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/test_e2e_watch.py; uv run ruff check tests/test_e2e_watch.py; uv run mypy --strict tests/test_e2e_watch.py | tail -1"}
