The module passed three runs in a row and the full gate is clean. `src` is unchanged, `tasks.md` is untouched and nothing is committed.\n\n- **Finding 1:** `test_line_precedes_any_edit_and_an_immediate_in_place_edit_is_recorded` is replaced by `test_in_place_edit_made_the_moment_the_line_appears_is_recorded`. It takes the start time and does the in-place `write_text` straight after `wait_ready()`, with no `history` call in between. It then requires the history to equal `IMPORTS + [set name demo→prod]` exactly. The docstring no longer says the import is recorded before the line.\n- **Finding 2:** I added `test_edit_after_any_idle_time_is_recorded_within_2_seconds`, split into 5 parallel cases. After each record it waits a different time before the next edit, so edits land at different points of the watcher's poll cycle. Together the cases cover waits from 0 to 2.4 s in 0.1 s steps (25 edits), and each edit must be recorded within 2 s as exactly one `set`.\n\nRerunning your 1-second-idle suggestion alone would not have caught the 2.3 s poll, because edits after a 1 s idle still get recorded in time. A sweep was needed. The cost is that the module now takes about 21 s instead of 10 s, and the full suite about 102 s instead of 77 s.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 5.1\n- TASK_BRIEF: Real `troupe config watch` subprocess. An in-place root edit made the moment `watching <dir>\n` appears (nothing in between) is recorded within 2 s, and the history is then exactly the import events plus that `set`. Edits after any idle time from 0 to 2.4 s (0.1 s steps) are each recorded within 2 s. Edits to the root, a YAML include, a nested include and a text include are each recorded within 2 s. Replace-by-rename is recorded. A `.yml`→`.yaml` rename records nothing and a later edit is recorded. Hand-added YAML and text includes give `set` events and are then followed. A hand-removed include gives `remove` events and later edits to it are ignored. Comment-only, formatting-only, touch, identical-bytes and unrelated-file edits record nothing within 2.5 s. Every test ends with SIGTERM: exit 0, stdout exactly the one line, stderr empty.\n- FILES_CHANGED: /work/repo/tests/test_e2e_watch.py (new, untracked)\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.4, 2.6, 2.8, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 4.6, 5.1, 5.2, 5.6, 5.7\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Components and Interfaces > Watcher (`config/watch.py`); Testing Strategy > E2E Tests > Watch; Testing Strategy > Timing Robustness\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT:\n  - Finding 1: temporary mutation in `handle_watch`, after `stdout.flush()`: `time.sleep(0.15); watcher.baseline = watcher._snapshot(watcher.watch_set)`. Running `uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/test_e2e_watch.py` three times gave "FAILED ...::test_in_place_edit_made_the_moment_the_line_appears_is_recorded / 1 failed, 22 passed" each time (3 of 3).\n  - Finding 2: `WATCH_POLL_SECONDS` temporarily set from 0.2 to 2.3. Three runs: run 1 "4 failed, 19 passed" (sweep bands [2] and [4], the moment-the-line-appears test, the hand-added YAML include test); run 2 "1 failed, 22 passed" (sweep band [1]); run 3 "3 failed, 20 passed" (sweep band [2], both hand-added include tests). At least one sweep band failed in every run.\n  - Round 1 mutations still apply: include files left out of the watch set gives 5 failures; always-sync plus always-differ reconcile gives all tests failing; a 5.0 s poll gives 9 failures.\n  - All mutations were reverted with `cp` and `git checkout`; `git diff src | wc -l` gives 0.\n- TESTS_RUN:\n  - `uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/test_e2e_watch.py` three times in a row: 23 passed in 21.28 s, 21.77 s and 20.95 s.\n  - `uv run pytest -n auto -q`: 2083 passed in 101.88 s.\n  - `uv run mypy --strict .`: Success, no issues found in 101 source files.\n  - `uv run ruff check .`: All checks passed.\n  - `uv run ruff format --check .`: 102 files already formatted.\n- CONCERNS:\n  - The sweep makes the module slower: about 21 s wall time under xdist, with the longest band idling about 7 s in total.\n  - The sweep catches poll intervals of about 2.3 s or more. An interval just over 2 s could still pass, because `wait_for_history` accepts a poll that starts before the deadline and the poll subprocess itself takes time.\n  - With a 2.3 s poll, which bands fail depends on timing. Each run fails at least one, but not always the same one.\n- EVIDENCE:\n  - `test_in_place_edit_made_the_moment_the_line_appears_is_recorded`: `wait_ready()`, then `since = time.monotonic()`, then `config.write_text(...)`, then `wait_for_history(len >= 6)`, then `events == [*IMPORTS, _set(6, "name", "demo", "prod")]`.\n  - `test_edit_after_any_idle_time_is_recorded_within_2_seconds[0-4]`: wait times are `0.1 * (band + 5 * step)` for 5 steps; each edit is an atomic root rewrite; asserts exactly `[_set(n, "name", previous, value)]` within 2 s of the edit.\n  - The other round 1 tests are unchanged, as listed before.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config watch`` racing other commands, failing loads, signals and clean-up, end to end.\n\nEvery test starts the real command in a subprocess through ``watch_support.WatchProcess`` and runs\nthe other commands (``set``, ``show``, ``history``) through the real console script. A positive\nassertion polls ``history`` (at most every 100 ms) against a 2 s deadline measured from the edit; a\nnegative assertion waits 2.5 s and then checks. ``history`` never reconciles, so it is a safe\nobserver. Edits are made straight after the readiness line, with no ``history`` call first, so a\nreadiness race is not hidden.\n\nEvery test ends by stopping ``watch`` with a signal and checking exit 0, stdout exactly the one\nreadiness line and no traceback (4.5, 4.6), and then the clean-up (4.9, 7.1-7.3): the only files\nanywhere under the test's directory are the files the test wrote, byte for byte (including an\ninclude outside ``.troupe/``), plus ``.troupe/history.sqlite``; no SQLite ``-wal``/``-shm`` side file\n(they exist only while the store is open, so their absence shows the store was closed), no lock,\ntemporary or other file, and a fresh ``history`` still reads the store.\n\nHand edits are written atomically (a temporary file renamed over the target), so neither ``watch``\nnor a racing command ever reads a half-written file.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport subprocess\nimport time\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import run_config, run_set\nfrom support import troupe_executable, write_theatre\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME, HISTORY_STORE_NAME\nfrom watch_support import HistoryEvent, WatchProcess\n\nNEGATIVE_WAIT_SECONDS = 2.5\n\"\"\"How long a test waits before asserting that nothing more was recorded.\"\"\"\n\nROOT = (\n    \"name: demo\\n\"\n    \"agents: file:agents.yml\\n\"\n    \"shared: file:../shared/team.yml\\n\"\n    \"prompt: file:prompt.txt\\...[truncated 19932 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''\n\ndef _unused(_: Callable[[], object]) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - keeps imports explicit\n    pass\n''','')\ns=s.replace(\"from collections.abc import Callable\\n\",\"\")\ns=s.replace(\"import os\\n\",\"import json\\nimport os\\n\",1)\ns=s.replace('''        \"\"\"A fresh ``troupe config history`` run (not throttled: never used in a polling loop).\"\"\"\n        import json\n\n''','''        \"\"\"A fresh ``troupe config history`` run (not throttled: never used in a polling loop).\"\"\"\n''')\ns=s.replace('''            theatre.written[\".troupe/troupe.yml\"] = theatre.path(\".troupe/troupe.yml\").read_bytes()\n''','''            content = ROOT.replace(\"name: demo\", f\"name: {by_set}\")\n            assert theatre.path(\".troupe/troupe.yml\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\") == content\n            theatre.written[\".troupe/troupe.yml\"] = content.encode(\"utf-8\")\n''')\ns=s.replace('''        assert watch.history() == expected\n        assert theatre.path(\".troupe/troupe.yml\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\") == ROOT.replace(\n            \"name: demo\", f\"name: {previous}\"\n        )\n        _stop(watch)''','''        assert watch.history() == expected\n        _stop(watch)''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py && uv run ruff check tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py && uv run mypy --strict tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py 2>&1 | tail -3; time uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py 2>&1 | tail -30"}

assistant: **Overall recommendation: GO.** The feature hangs together across tasks, every requirement has implementing code, and the full quality gate passes when re-run now: `uv run pytest -n auto` gave 2111 passed (no skips; uid is 1000, so the permission-based tests ran), `mypy --strict` reports no issues in 102 files, and `ruff check` / `ruff format --check` are clean. There are no Critical or Important findings. A few test-coverage gaps and small hardening items are listed below; none blocks.\n\n## E. Cross-task integration\n- **FYI – the chain is consistent.**\n  - `recorded_leaves` returns leaves sorted by `created_seq` (`src/troupe/config/recorded.py:42`). `reconcile_changes` re-sorts them anyway (`reconcile.py:45`), so it doesn't depend on its caller's ordering.\n  - `open_theatre` runs locate → lock → load → store → import → reconcile, then hands store and lock to `Theatre` with `pop_all()` (`theatre.py:93-106`). `Theatre.close` closes the store, then releases the lock in a `finally` (`theatre.py:55-58`).\n  - The Watcher opens and closes a theatre on every sync (`watch.py:94`), so the lock and store are held only during a sync.\n  - `handle_watch` installs the flag-only signal handlers before `Watcher.start()` and restores them in a `finally` (`cli/watch_command.py:46-60`).\n- **FYI – the `theatre_text` / `stream_handler` contract is consistent.**\n  - `_TheatreAction` and `set_defaults(theatre_text=...)` cover the typed argument, `$TROUPE_THEATRE` and the default (`cli/shell.py:51-89`).\n  - `run()` dispatches to the stream handler inside the shared `TroupeError` handler (`cli/main.py:118-126`).\n  - `handle_watch` writes to stdout only after `start()` succeeds (`watch_command.py:51-54`), so a start-up `TroupeError` leaves stdout empty.\n- **FYI – the `set` path is safe under the lock.** The setter's write → record → restore-on-failure (`writeback/setter.py:53-60`) runs entirely inside the lock. The Watcher therefore sees either the written-and-recorded bytes (empty diff) or the restored ones.\n- **FYI – store failures are retryable.** The store wraps `sqlite3`, eventsourcing, `OSError` and `ValueError` failures as `ConfigLoadError` (`store.py:40`). That is why `Watcher.run` catching only `TroupeError` is enough for 5.4.\n- **Minor – small edge cases.**\n  - `watch_command.py:53` encodes `theatre_text` with strict UTF-8. A directory argument that isn't valid UTF-8 (surrogate-escaped) would give a traceback after start-up instead of a clean error.\n  - Implementation Note 3.1 is still open: in `Theatre.__exit__`, an `OSError` from `lock.release` would replace the block's own error (`theatre.py:76-77`). Very unlikely.\n- No conflicting assumptions found.\n\n## F. Requirements coverage\nEvery ID from 1.1 to 7.3 is named in at least one test docstring, and each has implementing code:\n\n| Req | Implemented in |\n|-----|----------------|\n| 1 | `theatre.py:100-102`, `main.py:49` |\n| 2 | `recorded.py`, `reconcile.py` |\n| 3.1–3.3 | reconcile over the loaded document |\n| 3.4, 3.5 | `watch.py:92-105` |\n| 4 | `watch_command.py`, `shell.py` |\n| 5 | `watch.py` |\n| 6 | `lock.py` plus the idempotent diff |\n| 7 | `lock.py`, `theatre.py`, and the per-sync open/close in `watch.py` |\n\nPartial gaps:\n- **Minor – 6.2 "fails" case.** Only the *refused* `set` (exit 3) is tested while `watch` runs (`tests/test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py:304-327`). No test covers a `set` that *fails* (exit 4 on a write or history failure) while `watch` runs. The code path (restore under the lock) makes it safe.\n- **Minor – 4.7 unknown option.** Only a surplus argument is tested (`tests/test_cli_watch.py:100`). An unknown option goes through the same `TroupeArgumentParser.error`, so it is very likely correct, but nothing tests it for `watch`.\n- **Minor – 5.4.** It is named by a single test, which is in-process: `tests/test_watch_loop.py:623-648` (chmod 0 on the store). It only covers the "store cannot be opened" path. There is no end-to-end test and no "store write fails mid-transaction" case.\n- **FYI – 2.9 and 5.6** are each named by only 2 tests. That is adequate.\n\n## G. Design alignment\n- **FYI – dependency direction holds.** A grep finds no `troupe.cli` or `troupe.config.writeback` import in any core config module. Every core import points left, as the design requires: `recorded` → history, scalars; `reconcile` → store, recorded; `theatre` → reconcile, lock; `watch` → theatre.\n- **Minor – the boundary test checks only half the rule.** `tests/test_core_boundary.py` checks only for `writeback` imports, not `troupe.cli`. The grep is clean today, but the `troupe.cli` half of the rule has no test guarding it.\n- **Minor – File Structure Plan vs actual files.** All planned files exist. These test files are extra and not in the plan: `tests/test_theatre_sync.py`, `test_cli_streaming.py`, `test_cli_watch.py`, `test_e2e_watch_concurrency.py`, `test_core_boundary.py`. `watch.py:44` also has a private `_STOP_CHECK_SECONDS = 0.05` that isn't in `constants.py`. That is consistent with the design's "sleep in steps", but it is an undocumented constant.\n- **FYI – both design amendments are reflected in `design.md` and the code.**\n  - Removes without `ConfigPath.parse`: `reconcile.py:51-58`.\n  - Watcher baseline = loaded bytes: `watch.py:98,128-134`.\n  - No other drift.\n\n## G.5 Boundary audit\n- **OK – write-back logic unchanged.** `git diff 7629e92..HEAD -- src/troupe/config/writeback` shows only two changes. `compare.py` re-exports `scalar_equal` from `scalars` (the verbatim move), and `leaf_diff.py` has a docstring change. `set_command.py` is untouched; `set` simply runs under `open_theatre`'s lock and reconcile. `store.py` and `history.py` changes are docstrings only. No spillover.\n- **OK – revalidation triggers handled.** Two triggers fired: the `open_theatre` order change and the `scalar_equal` move. The full suite, including the config-foundation and config-set-writeback tests, passes today. Supersession notes were added to config-foundation (requirements, design, research, tasks, brief), to config-set-writeback (requirements, design, research, tasks), and to the roadmap. The six step-1 tests with the superseded expectation were rewritten.\n- **Minor – spec status not advanced.** `spec/spec/config-file-sync/spec.json` still says `"phase": "tasks-generated"`, even though the roadmap marks the feature `[x]`.\n\n## H. Implementation Notes\n- **Minor – 2.2 (fd leak on Ctrl-C).** A Ctrl-C while an ordinary command is blocked in `flock` leaks the fd until the process exits. It is harmless, and `watch` avoids it with flag-only handlers (`watch_command.py:38-40`).\n- **FYI – 2.2 (`O_DIRECTORY`).** The lock relies on `find_config_file` having run first, and it still does (`theatre.py:93-95`).\n- **FYI – 2.3 (stored paths).** `_stored_path` is the only place stored paths are turned back into `ConfigPath`s; nothing parses them elsewhere.\n- **Minor – 3.1.** The `Theatre.__exit__` hardening point is the same item listed under E; still open.\n- **FYI – 4.1, 5.1, 5.2.** These are testing lessons, and the tests reflect them: the X → transient → X regression test, editing straight after the readiness line, and the background `show` loops.\n\n## Markers and secrets\nA grep of the feature's new and changed source and test files finds no TBD, TODO, FIXME, HACK or XXX, and no secret-like patterns (keys, tokens, passwords, private keys).\n\n## Why GO\n- Integration across the tasks is consistent and the full gate passes.\n- The architecture and boundaries match the design, and write-back is unchanged apart from running under the lock.\n- The revalidation triggers were re-run and the related specs were annotated.\n\nRecommended small follow-ups, none of which blocks:\n1. An end-to-end test for a *failing* `set` (exit 4) while `watch` runs (6.2).\n2. An unknown-option test for `watch` (4.7).\n3. Extending `test_core_boundary.py` to also forbid `troupe.cli` imports.\n4. Updating the `spec.json` phase.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_cli_watch.py'; s=open(p).read()\na='''def test_missing_config_exits_4_naming_the_expected_file('''\nb='''def test_unknown_option_is_a_usage_error_without_a_line(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.7: an unknown option exits 2 under the usage rules and prints no ``watching`` line.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(tmp_path)\n\n    with WatchProcess(str(theatre), cwd=tmp_path, extra_args=[\"--no-such-option\"]) as watch:\n        assert watch.wait() == 2\n\n    assert watch.stdout == b\"\"\n    assert \"--no-such-option\" in watch.stderr\n    assert \"Traceback\" not in watch.stderr\n    assert not (theatre / CONFIG_DIR_NAME / HISTORY_STORE_NAME).exists()\n\n\ndef test_missing_config_exits_4_naming_the_expected_file('''\nassert a in s; s=s.replace(a,b,1); open(p,'w').write(s)\np='tests/test_core_boundary.py'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('WRITEBACK_PACKAGE = \"troupe.config.writeback\"\\n','WRITEBACK_PACKAGE = \"troupe.config.writeback\"\\nCLI_PACKAGE = \"troupe.cli\"\\n')\na='''def test_writeback_compare_still_exports_the_core_scalar_equal() -> None:'''\nb='''def test_no_core_config_module_imports_the_cli_package() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.5, 4.1: core configuration code (reconcile, lock, watcher) never depends on the CLI.\"\"\"\n    core_modules = sorted(CONFIG_PACKAGE.glob(\"*.py\"))\n    assert core_modules\n\n    offenders = {\n        module.name: imported\n        for module in core_modules\n        for imported in _imported_modules(module)\n        if imported == CLI_PACKAGE or imported.startswith(CLI_PACKAGE + \".\")\n    }\n\n    assert offenders == {}\n\n\n'''+a\nassert a in s; s=s.replace(a,b,1); open(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_cli_watch.py tests/test_core_boundary.py 2>&1 | tail -1; uv run mypy --strict . | tail -1; uv run ruff check . | tail -1; uv run ruff format --check . | tail -1"}

assistant: Step 2 ("Sync with files") is built and committed on `main`, and the final check is **GO**. On the final commit (`c89fc30`), 2113 tests pass, mypy and ruff are clean, and the working tree is clean. Nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened.\n\n## What changed\n- **Every command except `history` reconciles first.** It compares the files with what the history has recorded, leaf by leaf, and records a `set` or `remove` for each difference. Edits that only change formatting or comments record nothing.\n- **`history` never reconciles.** It still imports a theatre that has never been imported.\n- **`troupe config watch`:**\n  - prints `watching <dir>` only once it can already see changes;\n  - records each edit within 2 seconds, including edits to includes and includes added or removed by hand;\n  - reports a broken file once and recovers when it's fixed;\n  - exits 0 on SIGINT or SIGTERM.\n- **A per-theatre lock:** it uses the existing `.troupe/` directory, so no file is created. A `set` running alongside `watch` records exactly its own events and its write-back is never recorded again.\n\n## How it was done\nAll 12 tasks went through implementation, an independent review, and a fresh test run before each commit. The reviews caught three real problems:\n- **Task 2.3:** a removed leaf under an empty key would have made every later reconcile fail. Fixed, and the design updated.\n- **Task 4.1:** a file read halfway through a save could leave false `remove` events in the history that were never corrected. Fixed by comparing against the bytes the load actually read, with a regression test.\n- **Task 5.1:** the timing tests were too lenient to catch a readiness race or a too-slow polling interval. Strengthened, and shown to fail against both bugs.\n\n## Final validation\n- A real `watch` session in a temporary theatre covered a hand edit, a `set` alongside it, a hand-added include, a comment-only edit and SIGTERM. The history came out exactly as specified, stderr was empty, the exit code was 0, and `.troupe/` held only the config files and `history.sqlite`.\n- An independent audit found the code consistent with the design and its boundaries. Every one of the 44 requirements is covered by a test.\n- The step-1 specs and roadmap now say where step 2 replaces "hand edits add no events", and the six step-1 tests that asserted the old behaviour were rewritten in the same change as the new behaviour.\n- After the audit I added two small tests: `watch` with an unknown option, and core code never importing the CLI.\n\n## Known gaps\nNone of these blocks the feature:\n- Under requirement 6.2, a `set` that *fails* (exit 4) while `watch` runs has no end-to-end test. Only the *refused* case (exit 3) is tested.\n- If releasing the lock itself fails during an error, that release error would be reported instead of the original error. This is very unlikely.\n- Pressing Ctrl-C while an ordinary command waits for the lock leaves the lock's file handle open until that process exits, which happens immediately. It's harmless.\n- The full suite now takes about 3 minutes under `pytest -n auto`, mostly because of the timing-based `watch` tests.
